+# KEP-975: Partial Preemption of Elastic Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors](#story-1-spark-on-kubernetes-elastic-executors)
+    - [Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups](#story-2-ray-with-split-baselineopportunistic-worker-groups)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Two-phase model: request != release](#two-phase-model-request--release)
+  - [Workload API](#workload-api)
+  - [Opt-in annotation](#opt-in-annotation)
+  - [Reusing minCount](#reusing-mincount)
+  - [Scheduler / Preemption](#scheduler--preemption)
+    - [Eligibility](#eligibility)
+    - [Candidate ordering](#candidate-ordering)
+    - [Target selection](#target-selection)
+    - [Issuing preemptions](#issuing-preemptions)
+    - [No-fit behavior](#no-fit-behavior)
+  - [Webhook](#webhook)
+  - [Job runtime contract](#job-runtime-contract)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Kueue mutates the job's replica count directly](#kueue-mutates-the-jobs-replica-count-directly)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Today Kueue preempts a Workload as a whole: the victim is evicted, `stopJob` suspends it, and all
+of its Pods are torn down. For elastic workloads that can run at a reduced size (for example a Spark
+application whose executor count can shrink), whole eviction is unnecessarily disruptive.
+
+**Partial Preemption** lets Kueue reclaim quota from an elastic, opted-in Workload by asking it to
+scale one PodSet down to a selected target count at or above `minCount`, instead of evicting it.
+The scheduler selects the largest target count that lets the preemptor fit, so it reclaims only the
+replicas needed whenever possible. The victim keeps running at a smaller size and is never
+suspended. The feature is alpha and gated by the `PartialPreemption` feature gate; when the gate is
+off, behavior is identical to today.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a
+victim entirely. For a long-running elastic job this means killing the driver and every worker,
+losing in-flight progress, and paying a full restart — even though releasing a few replicas would
+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors
+
+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of
+suspending the whole Spark application or reducing it all the way to 2 executors, Kueue writes
+`reclaimTargetCount=10` on the executor PodSet. The Spark driver gracefully decommissions executors
+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- The victim must opt in (annotation), have exactly one PodSet with `minCount` set, and currently be
+  using more than `minCount`. Otherwise it is treated as a normal (whole-eviction) preemption target.
+- `reclaimTargetCount` is owned by Kueue and is not a user-set field. An in-flight target is kept
+  unchanged until the runtime converges and the Workload controller clears it.
+- Partial preemption interoperates with elastic-job scale-down. Admission `count` and
+  `resourceUsage` only decrease after the runtime updates the Workload spec and the Workload
+  controller completes the accounting update.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+- **Over-subscription window.** Naively releasing quota when the request is written would let the
+  preemptor and the not-yet-shrunk victim overlap. Mitigated by the [two-phase
+  model](#two-phase-model-request--release): the request is decoupled from the release; admission
+  accounting only drops after the runtime and Workload controller converge.
+- **Uncooperative runtime.** A runtime that ignores `reclaimTargetCount` never releases quota, so
+  the preemptor stays `Pending`. This is opt-in; only workloads whose runtime honors the field
+  should set the annotation. A future enhancement could fall back to whole eviction after a timeout.
+- **Feature isolation.** All behavior is behind the `PartialPreemption` gate; when off, the 
+  preemption path is the existing one.
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Two-phase model: request != release
+
+The signal and the quota release are intentionally decoupled to avoid any over-subscription window:
+
+- **request:** Kueue writes the selected `reclaimTargetCount` on the victim's admission. It does
+  **not** change the accounted `count` or `resourceUsage` and does **not** release quota.
+- **release:** The runtime reads `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+  count after the Pods converge. The Workload controller then updates the admission count and
+  resource usage and clears the target, thereby releasing quota.
+
+```mermaid
+sequenceDiagram
+    participant P as preemptor
+    participant K as Kueue
+    participant WL as victim Workload
+    participant D as job runtime
+    P->>K: needs quota, triggers preemption
+    K->>WL: request phase writes reclaimTargetCount, admission count unchanged
+    K-->>P: keep Pending
+    WL-->>D: runtime reads reclaimTargetCount
+    D->>D: shed excess replicas down to the target
+    D->>WL: update spec count after Pods converge
+    K->>WL: reduce admission count and resourceUsage, clear target
+    K->>P: admit preemptor after quota is released
+```
+
+### Workload API
+
+Add an optional, Kueue-owned field to `PodSetAssignment`:
+
+```go
+type PodSetAssignment struct {
+    // ...
+
+    // reclaimTargetCount, when set and lower than count, requests the elastic job to scale this
+    // PodSet down to reclaimTargetCount so partial preemption can reclaim its quota. Kueue owns
+    // this field; the job runtime reads it and sheds pods down to reclaimTargetCount. Kueue clears
+    // this field once the PodSet's desired spec count has converged to reclaimTargetCount or below.
+    // This is an alpha field and requires enabling the PartialPreemption feature gate.
+    //
+    // +optional
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Minimum=0
+    ReclaimTargetCount *int32 `json:"reclaimTargetCount,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### Opt-in annotation
+
+A Workload opts in via an annotation, e.g. `kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption: "true"`. The job's
+integration layer sets it on the Job; the jobframework reconciler propagates it onto the Workload so
+that the scheduler only ever needs to look at the Workload.
+
+### Reusing minCount
+
+Partial preemption reuses the existing `PodSet.MinCount` (see
+[KEP-420](/keps/420-partial-admission)) as the scale-down floor. The current partial-preemption
+algorithm requires exactly one PodSet with `minCount`; if there are zero or multiple such PodSets,
+the Workload falls back to normal whole-eviction handling.
+
+### Scheduler / Preemption
+
+#### Eligibility
+
+A candidate is eligible for partial preemption when: the `PartialPreemption` gate is enabled, the
+Workload carries the opt-in annotation with value `"true"`, exactly one PodSet has `minCount` set,
+and its effective used count is above `minCount`. The used count comes from
+`workload.Info.TotalRequests`; an existing lower `reclaimTargetCount` is used as the effective count
+for simulation.
+
+#### Candidate ordering
+
+Within the existing preemption candidate ordering, in-flight partial targets are considered early.
+After priority, an eligible partial-preemptible candidate is preferred over a whole-eviction-only
+candidate, and among partial-preemptible peers the candidate with higher effective used count is
+preferred. Priority still dominates, and with the gate off these criteria are no-ops.
+
+#### Target selection
+
+For an eligible candidate, the scheduler searches between the current effective used count and the

+Reductions can accumulate across multiple victims and repeated candidate rounds.
+
+The optional max-reclaimed-count annotation limits each reduction step, not the final accumulated
+reduction. An existing in-flight target is simulated and preserved until it clears. If partial
+reductions are still insufficient, a candidate that has reached `minCount` can later be handled as
+a normal whole-eviction candidate.
+
+#### Issuing preemptions
+
+For a partial target, Kueue does **not** evict or `stopJob`. It patches the victim's admission
+status to set `reclaimTargetCount` and emits a `PartiallyPreempted` event. Quota is not released
+here (that is the release phase of the two-phase model).
+
+#### No-fit behavior
+
+Partial targets are issued only when the resulting target set makes the preemptor fit. If the
+preemptor still does not fit, tentative partial and whole-eviction targets are discarded, no
+preemption is issued, and the preemptor remains `Pending` for a later scheduling cycle.
+
+### Webhook
+
+`reclaimTargetCount` lives under `status.admission`, which is otherwise immutable after admission.
+When the `PartialPreemption` gate is enabled, the Workload validating webhook allows
+`reclaimTargetCount` to change on an already-admitted Workload. When a target has completed, it also
+allows the Workload controller to clear the target and reduce admission `count` and `resourceUsage`
+to the converged spec count.
+
+### Job runtime contract
+
+Kueue only expresses intent; a cooperating runtime does the work. The contract for an integration:
+
+1. Set the opt-in annotation on the Job when the workload can tolerate partial preemption.
+2. Populate `minCount` for the elastic PodSet (the scale-down floor).
+3. Watch the Workload's `reclaimTargetCount` and cap the PodSet's desired size at
+   `min(desired, admittedCount, reclaimTargetCount)`.
+4. After the actual Pods converge to the target or below, lower the Workload spec count. The
+   Workload controller then updates admission accounting and clears the target.
```

  
  Thanks @zhengchenyu, agreed that the approaches are close.
  
  **Resize vs. slicing.** You're right: resize is the better long-term mechanism. @mimowo  guidance on #16233 was to adopt the mature workload slicing feature, and treat WorkloadResize as a separate piece of work for all job types ([comment](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/16233#issuecomment-5895631543)). Partial preemption isn't tied to slicing: the reclaim lowers the grant in place, and only grow-back adds a slice, so moving to WorkloadResize should need little change beyond grow-back. Until then, the overhead can perhaps be contained and managed by an object retention policy, or by deleting a replaced slice once its successor is admitted.
  
  **Why the driver stays unaware of Kueue in my reference implementation.** A key requirement for us is that partial preemption works with Spark 3.x, which most of our users run. A Kueue-aware driver requires changes to Spark core (e.g., KueueExecutorPodsAllocator - your work). Spark does not backport new features to maintenance branches, so this would not meet our objectives. Keeping the logic on the Kueue side should work with any Spark version. The one caveat is graceful decommissioning, which migrates shuffle data off reclaimed executors. It requires Spark 3.1 or higher and will not work in earlier versions.

+## Motivation
+
+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a
+victim entirely. For a long-running elastic job this means killing the driver and every worker,
+losing in-flight progress, and paying a full restart — even though releasing a few replicas would
+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors
+
+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of
+suspending the whole Spark application or reducing it all the way to 2 executors, Kueue writes
+`reclaimTargetCount=10` on the executor PodSet. The Spark driver gracefully decommissions executors
+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
```

  Adding 3 suggestions for additional real user stories from our team. Please feel to keep or ignore any of them.
  
  1 of 3
  ```suggestion
  
  #### Story 3: Reclaiming borrowed GPUs suspends a whole elastic training run
  
  As a user running TorchElastic training on a JobSet, I want my training run to use spare GPU quota in its Cohort.
  When another ClusterQueue reclaims part of that quota, I want the run to give back only the GPUs that are needed and continue at a smaller size.
  
  Each worker Pod uses a whole 8-GPU node and runs `torchrun --nnodes=4:16 --nproc-per-node=8`, so training can continue with 4 to 16 workers (32 to 128 GPUs).
  My JobSet opts in to partial preemption and declares a minimum of 4 workers.
  My ClusterQueue, `training`, shares a Cohort with `inference`; CPU and memory quota are not a constraint in this example:
  
  |                       | `training` | `inference` |
  |-----------------------|-----------:|------------:|
  | GPU `nominalQuota`    | 64         | 64          |
  | GPU `borrowingLimit`  | 64         | 0           |
  | `reclaimWithinCohort` | `Never`    | `Any`       |
  
  1. While `inference` is idle, my JobSet is admitted with 16 workers (128 GPUs): 64 GPUs from the nominal quota of `training` and 64 borrowed from `inference`.
  2. A 6-node batch inference job in `inference` needs 48 GPUs.
     Kueue asks my JobSet to shrink to 10 workers, not to 4.
  3. Six worker Pods terminate.
     TorchElastic re-forms the worker group and resumes training from the latest checkpoint with 10 workers.
  4. Kueue then admits the inference job.
  
  Training processes restart in step 3, so the run loses the progress made since its last checkpoint.
  
  Currently, Kueue suspends the whole JobSet in step 2.
  The JobSet is requeued with 16 workers and cannot be admitted again until 128 GPUs are free, even though 80 are available to `training`.
  
  > [!NOTE]
  > This story is forward-looking.
  > Kueue does not yet support elastic JobSets, so a JobSet cannot change size while it is admitted.
  > The story also needs JobSet's Alpha `ElasticJobSet` feature gate, which lets the worker count of a running JobSet change.
  > It describes the target behavior once a JobSet elastic integration exists; see [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats) for the integrations this KEP covers.
  
  ### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
  ```

+## Summary
+
+Today Kueue preempts a Workload as a whole: the victim is evicted, `stopJob` suspends it, and all
+of its Pods are torn down. For elastic workloads that can run at a reduced size (for example a Spark
+application whose executor count can shrink), whole eviction is unnecessarily disruptive.
+
+**Partial Preemption** lets Kueue reclaim quota from an elastic, opted-in Workload by asking it to
+scale one PodSet down to a selected target count at or above `minCount`, instead of evicting it.
+The scheduler selects the largest target count that lets the preemptor fit, so it reclaims only the
+replicas needed whenever possible. The victim keeps running at a smaller size and is never
+suspended. The feature is alpha and gated by the `PartialPreemption` feature gate; when the gate is
+off, behavior is identical to today.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a
+victim entirely. For a long-running elastic job this means killing the driver and every worker,
+losing in-flight progress, and paying a full restart — even though releasing a few replicas would
+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories

+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors
+
+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of
+suspending the whole Spark application or reducing it all the way to 2 executors, Kueue writes
+`reclaimTargetCount=10` on the executor PodSet. The Spark driver gracefully decommissions executors
+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats

  (See above comment)
  
  Story 2 of 3
  ```suggestion
  #### Story 4: Reclaiming GPU workers restarts a continuous Ray Data pipeline
  
  As a user running a continuous Ray Data pipeline as a RayJob, I want the pipeline to use whatever GPU quota is spare in its Cohort.
  When part of that quota is reclaimed, I want the Ray cluster and the pipeline to keep running with fewer GPU workers.
  
  The pipeline reads from an unbounded source, so it never completes and never releases its quota on its own.
  The Ray cluster has a CPU-only head, requesting 16 CPUs and 64 GiB of memory, and one worker group of GPU Pods.
  Each GPU worker uses a whole 8-GPU node.
  My RayJob opts in to partial preemption.
  My ClusterQueue, `data`, has no GPU nominal quota; every GPU the RayJob uses is borrowed.
  It shares a Cohort with `training`, which owns 16 GPU nodes; CPU and memory quota are not a constraint in this example:
  
  |                       | `data`  | `training` |
  |-----------------------|--------:|-----------:|
  | GPU `nominalQuota`    | 0       | 128        |
  | `reclaimWithinCohort` | `Never` | `Any`      |
  
  1. While `training` is idle, the RayJob runs with its head and 16 GPU workers (128 GPUs).
  2. A 12-node training job in `training` needs 96 GPUs.
     Kueue asks the RayJob to shrink the GPU worker group to 4 workers; the head keeps running.
  3. A 4-node evaluation job in `training` needs the remaining 32 GPUs.
     Removing every GPU worker would leave the head running with nothing to do, so Kueue evicts the whole RayJob, as it does today.
  
  Some pipelines keep state on the head that is expensive to rebuild, such as the driver's progress through the source.
  For those, I explicitly allow the GPU worker group to shrink to 0.
  In step 3, Kueue then removes the last 4 GPU workers instead: processing pauses, and the head keeps running until GPU nodes are free again.
  
  Currently, Kueue suspends the whole RayJob in step 2.
  KubeRay deletes the Ray cluster, and when the RayJob is admitted again, the cluster is recreated and the pipeline restarts from its last persisted progress.
  
  ### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
  ```

+# KEP-975: Partial Preemption of Elastic Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors](#story-1-spark-on-kubernetes-elastic-executors)
+    - [Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups](#story-2-ray-with-split-baselineopportunistic-worker-groups)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Two-phase model: request != release](#two-phase-model-request--release)
+  - [Workload API](#workload-api)
+  - [Opt-in annotation](#opt-in-annotation)
+  - [Reusing minCount](#reusing-mincount)
+  - [Scheduler / Preemption](#scheduler--preemption)
+    - [Eligibility](#eligibility)
+    - [Candidate ordering](#candidate-ordering)
+    - [Target selection](#target-selection)
+    - [Issuing preemptions](#issuing-preemptions)
+    - [No-fit behavior](#no-fit-behavior)
+  - [Webhook](#webhook)
+  - [Job runtime contract](#job-runtime-contract)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Kueue mutates the job's replica count directly](#kueue-mutates-the-jobs-replica-count-directly)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Today Kueue preempts a Workload as a whole: the victim is evicted, `stopJob` suspends it, and all
+of its Pods are torn down. For elastic workloads that can run at a reduced size (for example a Spark
+application whose executor count can shrink), whole eviction is unnecessarily disruptive.
+
+**Partial Preemption** lets Kueue reclaim quota from an elastic, opted-in Workload by asking it to
+scale one PodSet down to a selected target count at or above `minCount`, instead of evicting it.
+The scheduler selects the largest target count that lets the preemptor fit, so it reclaims only the
+replicas needed whenever possible. The victim keeps running at a smaller size and is never
+suspended. The feature is alpha and gated by the `PartialPreemption` feature gate; when the gate is
+off, behavior is identical to today.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a
+victim entirely. For a long-running elastic job this means killing the driver and every worker,
+losing in-flight progress, and paying a full restart — even though releasing a few replicas would
+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors
+
+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of
+suspending the whole Spark application or reducing it all the way to 2 executors, Kueue writes
+`reclaimTargetCount=10` on the executor PodSet. The Spark driver gracefully decommissions executors
+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- The victim must opt in (annotation), have exactly one PodSet with `minCount` set, and currently be
+  using more than `minCount`. Otherwise it is treated as a normal (whole-eviction) preemption target.
+- `reclaimTargetCount` is owned by Kueue and is not a user-set field. An in-flight target is kept
+  unchanged until the runtime converges and the Workload controller clears it.
+- Partial preemption interoperates with elastic-job scale-down. Admission `count` and
+  `resourceUsage` only decrease after the runtime updates the Workload spec and the Workload
+  controller completes the accounting update.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+- **Over-subscription window.** Naively releasing quota when the request is written would let the
+  preemptor and the not-yet-shrunk victim overlap. Mitigated by the [two-phase
+  model](#two-phase-model-request--release): the request is decoupled from the release; admission
+  accounting only drops after the runtime and Workload controller converge.
+- **Uncooperative runtime.** A runtime that ignores `reclaimTargetCount` never releases quota, so
+  the preemptor stays `Pending`. This is opt-in; only workloads whose runtime honors the field
+  should set the annotation. A future enhancement could fall back to whole eviction after a timeout.
+- **Feature isolation.** All behavior is behind the `PartialPreemption` gate; when off, the 
+  preemption path is the existing one.
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Two-phase model: request != release
+
+The signal and the quota release are intentionally decoupled to avoid any over-subscription window:
+
+- **request:** Kueue writes the selected `reclaimTargetCount` on the victim's admission. It does
+  **not** change the accounted `count` or `resourceUsage` and does **not** release quota.
+- **release:** The runtime reads `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+  count after the Pods converge. The Workload controller then updates the admission count and
+  resource usage and clears the target, thereby releasing quota.
+
+```mermaid
+sequenceDiagram
+    participant P as preemptor
+    participant K as Kueue
+    participant WL as victim Workload
+    participant D as job runtime
+    P->>K: needs quota, triggers preemption
+    K->>WL: request phase writes reclaimTargetCount, admission count unchanged
+    K-->>P: keep Pending
+    WL-->>D: runtime reads reclaimTargetCount
+    D->>D: shed excess replicas down to the target
+    D->>WL: update spec count after Pods converge
+    K->>WL: reduce admission count and resourceUsage, clear target
+    K->>P: admit preemptor after quota is released
+```
+
+### Workload API
+
+Add an optional, Kueue-owned field to `PodSetAssignment`:
+
+```go
+type PodSetAssignment struct {
+    // ...
+
+    // reclaimTargetCount, when set and lower than count, requests the elastic job to scale this
+    // PodSet down to reclaimTargetCount so partial preemption can reclaim its quota. Kueue owns
+    // this field; the job runtime reads it and sheds pods down to reclaimTargetCount. Kueue clears
+    // this field once the PodSet's desired spec count has converged to reclaimTargetCount or below.
+    // This is an alpha field and requires enabling the PartialPreemption feature gate.
+    //
+    // +optional
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Minimum=0
+    ReclaimTargetCount *int32 `json:"reclaimTargetCount,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### Opt-in annotation
+
+A Workload opts in via an annotation, e.g. `kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption: "true"`. The job's
+integration layer sets it on the Job; the jobframework reconciler propagates it onto the Workload so
+that the scheduler only ever needs to look at the Workload.
+
+### Reusing minCount
+
+Partial preemption reuses the existing `PodSet.MinCount` (see
+[KEP-420](/keps/420-partial-admission)) as the scale-down floor. The current partial-preemption
+algorithm requires exactly one PodSet with `minCount`; if there are zero or multiple such PodSets,
+the Workload falls back to normal whole-eviction handling.
+
+### Scheduler / Preemption
+
+#### Eligibility
+
+A candidate is eligible for partial preemption when: the `PartialPreemption` gate is enabled, the
+Workload carries the opt-in annotation with value `"true"`, exactly one PodSet has `minCount` set,
+and its effective used count is above `minCount`. The used count comes from
+`workload.Info.TotalRequests`; an existing lower `reclaimTargetCount` is used as the effective count
+for simulation.
+
+#### Candidate ordering
+
+Within the existing preemption candidate ordering, in-flight partial targets are considered early.
+After priority, an eligible partial-preemptible candidate is preferred over a whole-eviction-only
+candidate, and among partial-preemptible peers the candidate with higher effective used count is
+preferred. Priority still dominates, and with the gate off these criteria are no-ops.
+
+#### Target selection
+
+For an eligible candidate, the scheduler searches between the current effective used count and the
+allowed floor and selects the largest target count that makes the preemptor fit. If the candidate
+alone is insufficient, it is reduced to the current floor and later candidates can contribute.
+Reductions can accumulate across multiple victims and repeated candidate rounds.
+
+The optional max-reclaimed-count annotation limits each reduction step, not the final accumulated
+reduction. An existing in-flight target is simulated and preserved until it clears. If partial
+reductions are still insufficient, a candidate that has reached `minCount` can later be handled as
+a normal whole-eviction candidate.
+
+#### Issuing preemptions
+
+For a partial target, Kueue does **not** evict or `stopJob`. It patches the victim's admission
+status to set `reclaimTargetCount` and emits a `PartiallyPreempted` event. Quota is not released
+here (that is the release phase of the two-phase model).
+
```

  @khoover @zhengchenyu As discussed a bit earlier I think we need to explicitly decide on the behavior if a podset is allowed to scale down to 0. I think without this we run into 2 scenarios. 
  
  1) If we never fully evict when workers are scaled down to 0 and the head is running on a resource that has effectively infinite quota (think cpu head gpu workers) we run into an issue where we always leave cluster heads running with no work being done leading to unnecessary resource burn. 
  2) As discussed in story 2, we might have an opportunistic worker group that can scale to 0 where we do not want the whole job to be evicted just because it is at a mincount of 0. Another example could be a RayCluster used as a development workspace runs a notebook on the CPU-only head and GPU tasks on the workers. When the GPUs are reclaimed, the user keeps working on CPU and waits for GPU workers to come back. Kueue should shrink the worker group to 0 and keep the head running.
  
  **Proposal:** add a `kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption-zero-count-strategy` annotation that decides what happens when partial preemption would shrink the elastic PodSet to 0: `evict` the whole Workload (default) or `keepRunning` the other PodSets.
  
  ````suggestion
  
  #### Zero-count strategy
  
  An elastic PodSet with `minCount` 0 can be shrunk to 0 Pods.
  Whether the rest of the Workload should keep running then depends on the workload:
  
  - If the other PodSets only coordinate the workers, such as a Ray head, they keep running with nothing to do and unnecessarily burn resources. This is worse when the head runs on a resource with effectively unlimited quota, because nothing ever needs that quota back, so the Workload is never preempted.
  - If the PodSet is opportunistic extra capacity (Story 2), or the other PodSets do their own work
  , such as a notebook on the head of a development workspace, the Workload should keep running and
   wait for the workers to come back.
  
  The `kueue.x-k8s.io/podset-partial-preemption-zero-count-strategy` annotation on the PodSet's template decides what Kueue does in that case:
  
  - `evict` (default): Kueue preempts the whole Workload instead of shrinking the PodSet to 0.
  - `keepRunning`: Kueue shrinks the PodSet to 0, and the other PodSets keep running.
  
  In this example, Kueue can shrink `spot` to 0 while the head and `main` keep running.
  If `main` would reach 0, Kueue preempts the whole RayCluster.
  
  ```yaml
  apiVersion: ray.io/v1
  kind: RayCluster
  metadata:
    annotations:
      kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption: "true"
  spec:
    headGroupSpec:                # CPU-only head, never shrunk
      template:
        spec:
          containers:
          - name: ray-head
            resources:
              requests: {cpu: "16"}
    workerGroupSpecs:
    - groupName: main             # core GPU workers; the job is useless without them
      minReplicas: 0
      maxReplicas: 8
      template:
        metadata:
          annotations:
            kueue.x-k8s.io/podset-partial-preemption-zero-count-strategy: evict
        spec:
          containers:
          - name: ray-worker
            resources:
              limits: {nvidia.com/gpu: "8"}
    - groupName: spot             # opportunistic extra GPU workers
      minReplicas: 0
      maxReplicas: 8
      template:
        metadata:
          annotations:
            kueue.x-k8s.io/podset-partial-preemption-zero-count-strategy: keepRunning
        spec:
          containers:
          - name: ray-worker
            resources:
              limits: {nvidia.com/gpu: "8"}
  ```
  
  Only `evict` and `keepRunning` are accepted.
  The annotation has no effect when the PodSet's `minCount` is greater than 0, or when the Workload has not opted in to partial preemption.
  ````

+# KEP-975: Partial Preemption of Elastic Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors](#story-1-spark-on-kubernetes-elastic-executors)
+    - [Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups](#story-2-ray-with-split-baselineopportunistic-worker-groups)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Two-phase model: request != release](#two-phase-model-request--release)
+  - [Workload API](#workload-api)
+  - [Opt-in annotation](#opt-in-annotation)
+  - [Reusing minCount](#reusing-mincount)
+  - [Scheduler / Preemption](#scheduler--preemption)
+    - [Eligibility](#eligibility)
+    - [Candidate ordering](#candidate-ordering)
+    - [Target selection](#target-selection)
+    - [Issuing preemptions](#issuing-preemptions)
+    - [No-fit behavior](#no-fit-behavior)
+  - [Webhook](#webhook)
+  - [Job runtime contract](#job-runtime-contract)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Kueue mutates the job's replica count directly](#kueue-mutates-the-jobs-replica-count-directly)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Today Kueue preempts a Workload as a whole: the victim is evicted, `stopJob` suspends it, and all
+of its Pods are torn down. For elastic workloads that can run at a reduced size (for example a Spark
+application whose executor count can shrink), whole eviction is unnecessarily disruptive.
+
+**Partial Preemption** lets Kueue reclaim quota from an elastic, opted-in Workload by asking it to
+scale one PodSet down to a selected target count at or above `minCount`, instead of evicting it.
+The scheduler selects the largest target count that lets the preemptor fit, so it reclaims only the
+replicas needed whenever possible. The victim keeps running at a smaller size and is never
+suspended. The feature is alpha and gated by the `PartialPreemption` feature gate; when the gate is
+off, behavior is identical to today.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a
+victim entirely. For a long-running elastic job this means killing the driver and every worker,
+losing in-flight progress, and paying a full restart — even though releasing a few replicas would
+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors
+
+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of
+suspending the whole Spark application or reducing it all the way to 2 executors, Kueue writes
+`reclaimTargetCount=10` on the executor PodSet. The Spark driver gracefully decommissions executors
+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- The victim must opt in (annotation), have exactly one PodSet with `minCount` set, and currently be
```

   Why exactly one PodSet? Imagine a RayCluster with data-processing workers and elastic training workers. The two groups use different resource flavors, so they should be able to scale "independently" of each other (in quotes because in practice this can be a hard problem). If your job is configured so that both groups can scale independently, Kueue should allow each of them to be partially preempted.

+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one

+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is

+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down

```

  [Nit] Multi-host Ray worker groups (`numOfHosts > 1`) should be a non-goal, as they are for partial scale-up ([KEP-12100](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/blob/0b2f1029ac7a59be7965f559c04ff48d182c799f/keps/12100-partial-scaleup-for-elasticjob/README.md#L62)).
  Each replica is several Pods, such as a TPU slice across 2 hosts, so Ray can only reach targets that are multiples of `numOfHosts`.
  
  ````suggestion
  - Multi-host Ray worker groups (`numOfHosts > 1`) are not supported, as in partial scale-up
    (KEP-12100): Ray can only reach targets that are multiples of `numOfHosts`.
  ````

+## Summary

+Today Kueue preempts a Workload as a whole: the victim is evicted, `stopJob` suspends it, and all

+suspended. The feature is alpha and gated by the `PartialPreemption` feature gate; when the gate is
+off, behavior is identical to today.
+
+## Motivation

+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a

+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors

+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of

+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
```

  Story 3 of 3 
  ````suggestion
  #### Story 5: Preempting an interactive Ray cluster loses the user's notebook session
  
  As a researcher, I use a RayCluster as an interactive workspace: a Jupyter notebook runs on the head, and I submit Ray tasks to GPU workers from it.
  When a higher-priority job needs GPUs, I want to give back GPU workers, not lose my session.
  Partial preemption must never disrupt the head: it runs my notebook server, and I need it running to save my development work.
  
  The head requests 8 CPUs and 32 GiB of memory and runs the notebook server.
  The GPU worker group uses the Ray autoscaler, and each GPU worker uses a whole 8-GPU node.
  My RayCluster opts in to partial preemption and declares a minimum of 0 GPU workers.
  It runs at a lower priority than production jobs in the same ClusterQueue, `research`; CPU and memory quota are not a constraint in this example:
  
  | `research`              |                                            |
  |-------------------------|--------------------------------------------|
  | GPU `nominalQuota`      | 64                                         |
  | `withinClusterQueue`    | `LowerPriority`                            |
  | WorkloadPriorityClasses | `interactive` (100), `production` (1000)   |
  
  1. My RayCluster runs at priority `interactive` with its head and 4 GPU workers (32 GPUs).
     A production fine-tuning job uses the other 32 GPUs.
  2. A second production job needs 16 GPUs.
     Kueue asks my RayCluster to shrink the GPU worker group to 2 workers; the head keeps running.
  3. Two GPU worker Pods terminate.
     My notebook kernel and the results it has already collected are kept, and Ray recomputes task results that were stored only on the removed workers.
  4. When the fine-tuning job finishes, the autoscaler scales the GPU workers back up as my notebook submits more work.
  
  Currently, Kueue suspends the whole RayCluster in step 2.
  KubeRay deletes the head with the workers, so the notebook server goes down, unsaved work is lost, and so is everything in the kernel's memory.
  The RayCluster is requeued with 4 GPU workers and cannot start again until 32 GPUs are free.
  
  ### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
  ````

+The scheduler selects the largest target count that lets the preemptor fit, so it reclaims only the
+replicas needed whenever possible. The victim keeps running at a smaller size and is never
+suspended. The feature is alpha and gated by the `PartialPreemption` feature gate; when the gate is
+off, behavior is identical to today.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a
+victim entirely. For a long-running elastic job this means killing the driver and every worker,
+losing in-flight progress, and paying a full restart — even though releasing a few replicas would
+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors
+
+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of
+suspending the whole Spark application or reducing it all the way to 2 executors, Kueue writes
+`reclaimTargetCount=10` on the executor PodSet. The Spark driver gracefully decommissions executors
+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
```

  > The JobSet is requeued with 16 workers and cannot be admitted again until 128 GPUs are free, even though 80 are available to `training`.
  
  Since you use `torchrun --nnodes=4:16`, this issue should be addressed through "partial admission", shouldn't it?
  
  Restart train job has two problem:
  * If the job is partial preempted, the process group still change, the torchrun still need to reconstruct process group and reload the last checkpoint. "partial preempt" can not slove this problem. However, if we use dynamic process group such as tochft, I think that "partial preempt" is usefull.
  * You said that "cannot be admitted again until 128 GPUs are free, even though 80 are available to `training`". I think it is should be sloved by partial admission. If we use minCount=4 and partial admission is true, we can re-amdit with 10 workers.

@@ -0,0 +1,294 @@
+# KEP-975: Partial Preemption of Elastic Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors](#story-1-spark-on-kubernetes-elastic-executors)
+    - [Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups](#story-2-ray-with-split-baselineopportunistic-worker-groups)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Two-phase model: request != release](#two-phase-model-request--release)
+  - [Workload API](#workload-api)
+  - [Opt-in annotation](#opt-in-annotation)
+  - [Reusing minCount](#reusing-mincount)
+  - [Scheduler / Preemption](#scheduler--preemption)
+    - [Eligibility](#eligibility)
+    - [Candidate ordering](#candidate-ordering)
+    - [Target selection](#target-selection)
+    - [Issuing preemptions](#issuing-preemptions)
+    - [No-fit behavior](#no-fit-behavior)
+  - [Webhook](#webhook)
+  - [Job runtime contract](#job-runtime-contract)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+  - [Kueue mutates the job's replica count directly](#kueue-mutates-the-jobs-replica-count-directly)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+Today Kueue preempts a Workload as a whole: the victim is evicted, `stopJob` suspends it, and all
+of its Pods are torn down. For elastic workloads that can run at a reduced size (for example a Spark
+application whose executor count can shrink), whole eviction is unnecessarily disruptive.
+
+**Partial Preemption** lets Kueue reclaim quota from an elastic, opted-in Workload by asking it to
+scale one PodSet down to a selected target count at or above `minCount`, instead of evicting it.
+The scheduler selects the largest target count that lets the preemptor fit, so it reclaims only the
+replicas needed whenever possible. The victim keeps running at a smaller size and is never
+suspended. The feature is alpha and gated by the `PartialPreemption` feature gate; when the gate is
+off, behavior is identical to today.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Elastic batch/data workloads (Spark, Ray, and similar) can often continue making progress
+with fewer replicas. When a higher-priority Workload needs quota, Kueue's only tool is to evict a
+victim entirely. For a long-running elastic job this means killing the driver and every worker,
+losing in-flight progress, and paying a full restart — even though releasing a few replicas would
+have been enough.
+
+Kueue cannot shrink a running job on its own: only the job's controller/driver owns its Pods.
+Partial preemption therefore needs a way for Kueue to **signal** a desired reduced size and for the
+job runtime to **act** on it, while the quota accounting stays correct throughout.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Provide an opt-in mechanism where preemption of an elastic Workload reclaims quota by scaling one
+  of its PodSets down towards `minCount`, rather than evicting the whole Workload.
+- Guarantee no quota over-subscription at any point during the scale-down.
+- Keep the mechanism framework-agnostic in Kueue: Kueue only expresses intent; any controller/driver
+  that honors the signal can benefit.
+- Be fully gated so that, with the gate disabled, there is zero behavioral change.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Kueue does not itself terminate Pods or mutate the parent job's spec; the job runtime is
+  responsible for shedding replicas (see [Alternatives](#alternatives) for why).
+- Kueue does not guarantee the job respects the requested reduced size; a cooperating runtime is
+  assumed (opt-in).
+- Autoscaling / scaling back up after preemption is out of scope; this KEP only covers scale-down
+  for quota reclamation.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Introduce a per-PodSet target, `status.admission.podSetAssignments[].reclaimTargetCount`, that Kueue
+writes on an admitted elastic Workload to request that the PodSet scale down to that count. The job
+runtime observes `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds the excess replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+count after the scale-down converges. The Workload controller then reduces the admission count and
+resource usage and clears `reclaimTargetCount`, releasing quota for the preemptor.
+
+The preemption decision path (both the classical and the Fair Sharing algorithms) is extended so
+that an eligible elastic victim is simulated at the selected reduced count in the scheduling
+snapshot instead of being removed, and such victims are preferred over whole eviction of
+equal-priority peers.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: Spark on Kubernetes elastic executors
+
+A Spark application runs with 20 executors (`minExecutors=2`) in a low-priority ClusterQueue that is
+borrowing quota. A high-priority job arrives and needs quota equivalent to 10 executors. Instead of
+suspending the whole Spark application or reducing it all the way to 2 executors, Kueue writes
+`reclaimTargetCount=10` on the executor PodSet. The Spark driver gracefully decommissions executors
+down to 10 and updates the Workload spec count. The Workload controller then updates admission
+accounting, releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The Spark application keeps
+running the entire time.
+
+#### Story 2: Ray with split baseline/opportunistic worker groups
+
+A RayCluster runs with two worker groups in a low-priority ClusterQueue with nominal quota and a
+borrowing limit. One worker group is baseline capacity, sized to run only using the queue's nominal
+quota, while the other is a `0..N` scalable group for opportunistic extra capacity. A high-priority
+job arrives and needs part of the borrowed quota. Instead of suspending the entire RayCluster,
+killing the head and all workers, Kueue writes `reclaimTargetCount=0` on the opportunistic worker
+group's PodSet. The Ray driver scales in that worker group and updates the Workload spec count. The
+Workload controller then releases the quota, and the high-priority job is admitted. The RayCluster,
+including its head and baseline worker group, continues running the entire time.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- The victim must opt in (annotation), have exactly one PodSet with `minCount` set, and currently be
+  using more than `minCount`. Otherwise it is treated as a normal (whole-eviction) preemption target.
+- `reclaimTargetCount` is owned by Kueue and is not a user-set field. An in-flight target is kept
+  unchanged until the runtime converges and the Workload controller clears it.
+- Partial preemption interoperates with elastic-job scale-down. Admission `count` and
+  `resourceUsage` only decrease after the runtime updates the Workload spec and the Workload
+  controller completes the accounting update.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+- **Over-subscription window.** Naively releasing quota when the request is written would let the
+  preemptor and the not-yet-shrunk victim overlap. Mitigated by the [two-phase
+  model](#two-phase-model-request--release): the request is decoupled from the release; admission
+  accounting only drops after the runtime and Workload controller converge.
+- **Uncooperative runtime.** A runtime that ignores `reclaimTargetCount` never releases quota, so
+  the preemptor stays `Pending`. This is opt-in; only workloads whose runtime honors the field
+  should set the annotation. A future enhancement could fall back to whole eviction after a timeout.
+- **Feature isolation.** All behavior is behind the `PartialPreemption` gate; when off, the 
+  preemption path is the existing one.
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Two-phase model: request != release
+
+The signal and the quota release are intentionally decoupled to avoid any over-subscription window:
+
+- **request:** Kueue writes the selected `reclaimTargetCount` on the victim's admission. It does
+  **not** change the accounted `count` or `resourceUsage` and does **not** release quota.
+- **release:** The runtime reads `reclaimTargetCount`, sheds replicas, and updates the Workload spec
+  count after the Pods converge. The Workload controller then updates the admission count and
+  resource usage and clears the target, thereby releasing quota.
+
+```mermaid
+sequenceDiagram
+    participant P as preemptor
+    participant K as Kueue
+    participant WL as victim Workload
+    participant D as job runtime
+    P->>K: needs quota, triggers preemption
+    K->>WL: request phase writes reclaimTargetCount, admission count unchanged
+    K-->>P: keep Pending
+    WL-->>D: runtime reads reclaimTargetCount
+    D->>D: shed excess replicas down to the target
+    D->>WL: update spec count after Pods converge
+    K->>WL: reduce admission count and resourceUsage, clear target
+    K->>P: admit preemptor after quota is released
+```
+
+### Workload API
+
+Add an optional, Kueue-owned field to `PodSetAssignment`:
+
+```go
+type PodSetAssignment struct {
+    // ...
+
+    // reclaimTargetCount, when set and lower than count, requests the elastic job to scale this
+    // PodSet down to reclaimTargetCount so partial preemption can reclaim its quota. Kueue owns
+    // this field; the job runtime reads it and sheds pods down to reclaimTargetCount. Kueue clears
+    // this field once the PodSet's desired spec count has converged to reclaimTargetCount or below.
+    // This is an alpha field and requires enabling the PartialPreemption feature gate.
+    //
+    // +optional
+    // +kubebuilder:validation:Minimum=0
+    ReclaimTargetCount *int32 `json:"reclaimTargetCount,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+### Opt-in annotation
+
+A Workload opts in via an annotation, e.g. `kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption: "true"`. The job's
+integration layer sets it on the Job; the jobframework reconciler propagates it onto the Workload so
+that the scheduler only ever needs to look at the Workload.
+
+### Reusing minCount
+
+Partial preemption reuses the existing `PodSet.MinCount` (see
+[KEP-420](/keps/420-partial-admission)) as the scale-down floor. The current partial-preemption
+algorithm requires exactly one PodSet with `minCount`; if there are zero or multiple such PodSets,
+the Workload falls back to normal whole-eviction handling.
+
+### Scheduler / Preemption
+
+#### Eligibility
+
+A candidate is eligible for partial preemption when: the `PartialPreemption` gate is enabled, the
+Workload carries the opt-in annotation with value `"true"`, exactly one PodSet has `minCount` set,
+and its effective used count is above `minCount`. The used count comes from
+`workload.Info.TotalRequests`; an existing lower `reclaimTargetCount` is used as the effective count
+for simulation.
+
+#### Candidate ordering
+
+Within the existing preemption candidate ordering, in-flight partial targets are considered early.
+After priority, an eligible partial-preemptible candidate is preferred over a whole-eviction-only
+candidate, and among partial-preemptible peers the candidate with higher effective used count is
+preferred. Priority still dominates, and with the gate off these criteria are no-ops.
+
+#### Target selection
+
+For an eligible candidate, the scheduler searches between the current effective used count and the
+allowed floor and selects the largest target count that makes the preemptor fit. If the candidate
+alone is insufficient, it is reduced to the current floor and later candidates can contribute.
+Reductions can accumulate across multiple victims and repeated candidate rounds.
+
+The optional max-reclaimed-count annotation limits each reduction step, not the final accumulated
+reduction. An existing in-flight target is simulated and preserved until it clears. If partial
+reductions are still insufficient, a candidate that has reached `minCount` can later be handled as
+a normal whole-eviction candidate.
+
+#### Issuing preemptions
+
+For a partial target, Kueue does **not** evict or `stopJob`. It patches the victim's admission
+status to set `reclaimTargetCount` and emits a `PartiallyPreempted` event. Quota is not released
+here (that is the release phase of the two-phase model).
+
```

  If we set minCount to 0, is it possible to resolve this issue? Because only when count <= minCount, we will trigger full eviction. So we can set minCount to 0.
  
  If `kueue.x-k8s.io/partial-preemption-zero-count-strategy` is `keepRunning`, I am not sure how to handle? The job will not be full eviction, is it ok?

  But it is a limitation of the current main branch, which allows only one PodSet to have minCount set. We need to develop support for this feature. We also want this feature!
